\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\geq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for some }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt]

\displaystyle\leq\,\sum_{n\in\hskip 0.40974pt\mathbb{N}}\,\mathbb{P}[\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\geq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\hskip 0.50003pt]

(95)

\displaystyle\leq\,2\hskip-1.00006pt\sum_{n\in\hskip 0.40974pt\mathbb{N}}\,\exp(-Cn^{s})\hskip 1.00006pt,

where
C=2\hskip 0.50003ptc^{\hskip 0.40974pt2}/(b-a)^{-2}
and
s=1-2\hskip 0.50003ptr
.
The last sum in (
95
) converges if and only if
C>0
and
s>0
, which is equivalent to
c>0
and
r<1/2
, what is assumed to be true in the following.
Since
x\mapsto\exp(-Cx^{s})
is monotonically decreasing on
[\hskip 0.24994pt0,\infty)
we can bound the sum by its integral equivalent and use the substitution
t=Cx^{s}
to obtain

\sum_{n\in\hskip 0.40974pt\mathbb{N}}\,\exp(-Cn^{s})\,\leq\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-1.99997pt\exp(-Cx^{s})\;dx\,=\,C^{\hskip 0.40974pt-1/s}\frac{\Gamma(1/s)}{s}\hskip 1.00006pt.

(96)

Furthermore, a simple transformation yields that

2\hskip 0.50003ptC^{\hskip 0.40974pt-1/s}\frac{\Gamma(1/s)}{s}\,\leq\,\beta\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{2\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pts}\,\leq\,C\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\sqrt{\hskip-0.50003pt\frac{(b-a)^{2}}{\hskip-3.00003pt2}\hskip-1.49994pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{2\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pts}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\leq\,c\hskip 1.00006pt,

such that in combination with (
95
) and (
96
) we have

\mathbb{P}[\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\,\geq\,c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for some }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,\leq\,\beta\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad c\,\geq\,\sqrt{\hskip-0.50003pt\frac{(b-a)^{2}}{\hskip-3.00003pt2}\hskip-1.49994pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{2\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pts}\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip 1.00006pt.

(97)

The first part of the statement now follows by taking the complement of the event in (
97
).
The second part of the statement follows since

\frac{(b-a)^{2}}{\hskip-3.00003pt2}\hskip-1.49994pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{2\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pts}\,\leq\,\frac{(b-a)^{2}}{\hskip-3.00003pt2^{\hskip 0.2048pts}}\hskip-1.49994pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{2\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pts}\,=\,(b-a)^{2}\hskip-1.49994pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pts}\hskip-1.00006pt,

where we used that
s=1-2\hskip 0.50003ptr\in(0,1]
.
∎

We note that for
\Sigma
-measurable functions
g_{\hskip 0.2048pt1}
and
g_{2}
and
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}
we have that

\{g_{\hskip 0.2048pt1}+g_{2}\,\leq\,\varepsilon\}\,\supseteq\,\{g_{\hskip 0.2048pt1}\,\leq\,\lambda\hskip 0.50003pt\varepsilon\}\,\cap\,\{g_{2}\,\leq\,(1-\lambda)\hskip 0.50003pt\varepsilon\}\hskip 1.00006pt.

(98)

The statement in (
98
) will be used multiple times later on and is easier to grasp in this simplistic form.

Lemma 20

Let
\mathbf{Y}
be a random variable such that
a\leq\mathbf{Y}\leq b
almost surely.
Then, for all
r\in[\hskip 0.24994pt0,1/2)
and for all
\beta\in(0,1]
there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})\hskip-1.00006pt-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{V}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\,\leq\,c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right]\,\geq\,1-\beta\hskip 1.00006pt.

In particular, writing
s=1-2\hskip 0.50003ptr
, we can choose

c\,=\,(b-a)^{2}\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt1+\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{2\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-2.04861pts/2}\right)^{\hskip-2.25342pt2}\hskip-1.00006pt.

Proof.
.

First, we define the random variable
\mathbf{Z}=(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt)^{2}
and note that
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Z}]=\mathbb{V}[\mathbf{Y}]
. Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Z})\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert

\displaystyle\,=\,\left\lvert\,\frac{1}{n}\hskip 0.50003pt\sum_{i\hskip 0.40974pt=1}^{n}\left((\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{Y}_{\hskip-0.81949pti}-\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y}))^{2}-(\mathbf{Y}_{\hskip-0.81949pti}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt)^{2}\hskip 0.50003pt\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\,=\,\left\lvert\,\frac{1}{n}\hskip 0.50003pt\sum_{i\hskip 0.40974pt=1}^{n}\left(\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})^{2}-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]^{2}-2\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{Y}_{\hskip-1.22911pti}(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt)\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\,=\,\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})+\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt)(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt)-2\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}])\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert

\displaystyle\,=\,\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert^{2}

such that

\displaystyle\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mathbb{V}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\,\leq\,\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Z})\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert+\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Z})-\mathbb{V}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\,=\,\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert^{2}+\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Z})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Z}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\hskip 1.00006pt.

Hence, setting

x\,\coloneqq\,\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{2\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pts}\quad\text{ and }\quad c\,\coloneqq\,(b-a)^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt\left(x+\frac{\sqrt{x\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip 3.00003pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt4}\right)\hskip-1.00006pt,

where
s=1-2\hskip 0.50003ptr
, and using (
98
) we have

\displaystyle\left\{\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mathbb{V}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\leq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\left\{\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert^{2}+\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Z})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Z}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\leq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\left\{\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert^{2}\leq(b-a)^{2}x\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}\cap\left\{\hskip-0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Z})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Z}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\leq\frac{(b-a)^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt}{\hskip-1.49994pt4\hskip 1.49994pt}\sqrt{x}n^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\hskip-0.50003pt\right\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\left\{\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\leq(b-a)\sqrt{x}\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}\cap\left\{\hskip-0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Z})-\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Z}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\leq\frac{(b-a)^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt}{\hskip-1.49994pt4\hskip 1.49994pt}\sqrt{x}n^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\hskip-0.50003pt\right\}

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, such that taking the intersection over all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and applying Lemma
B.4
we obtain that

\mathbb{P}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mathbb{V}[\mathbf{Y}]\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\leq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right]\,\geq\,\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)+\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)-1\,=\,1-\beta\hskip 1.00006pt,

where we used that
\mathbf{Z}\in[0,(b-a)^{2}/4]
.
The second part of the statement holds since

x+\frac{\sqrt{x\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip 3.00003pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt4}\,\leq\,x+2\sqrt{x}+1\,=\,\left(\sqrt{x}+1\right)^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt,

such that overall the claim follows.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Proposition
5.1
itself.
Recall that here
\mathbf{Y}\sim\Unif(a,b)
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

First, we set

x\,\coloneqq\,\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{8\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pts}\quad\text{and}\quad c\,\coloneqq\,(b-a)\sqrt{x}+6(b-a)(1+\sqrt{x})^{2}\hskip 1.00006pt,

where
s=1-2\hskip 0.50003ptr
, and use (
98
) to obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-7.5pt\left\{\lvert\mathbf{A}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}^{\hskip-1.22911pt\text{MB}}\hskip-0.50003pt-a|\leq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\left\{\lvert\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mu\rvert+\sqrt{3\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\left\lvert\sqrt{\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})}-\sigma\right\rvert\leq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}

(99)

\displaystyle\supseteq\left\{\lvert\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mu\rvert\leq(b-a)\sqrt{x}n^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\sigma^{2}\right\rvert\leq\frac{6(b-a)(1+\sqrt{x})^{2}\sigma}{\sqrt{3}}\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\left\{\lvert\mathbf{E}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\mu\rvert\leq(b-a)\sqrt{x}n^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\sigma^{2}\right\rvert\leq(b-a)^{2}(1+\sqrt{x})^{2}\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\right\}\hskip-1.00006pt,

where for the second inclusion we used that

\left\lvert\sqrt{\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn\hskip 0.81949pt}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})}-\sigma\right\rvert\,=\,\frac{\left\lvert\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\sigma^{2}\right\rvert}{\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})+\sigma}\,\leq\,\frac{\left\lvert\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-\sigma^{2}\right\rvert}{\sigma}

since
(\sqrt{\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{X})\hskip 1.00006pt}-\sigma)(\sqrt{\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{X})\hskip 1.00006pt}+\sigma)=\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{X})-\sigma^{2}
and
\sqrt{\mathbf{V}_{\hskip-1.22911ptn}\hskip-0.50003pt(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{X})\hskip 1.00006pt}\geq 0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, forming the intersection over all
n\in\mathbb{N}
of (
99
) and using Lemma
B.4
and Lemma
B.4
, we obtain that

\mathbb{P}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{A}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}^{\hskip-1.22911pt\text{MB}}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-a\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\leq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right]\,\geq\,\left(1-\frac{\beta}{8}\right)+\left(1-\frac{\beta}{4}\right)-1\,\geq\,1-\frac{\beta}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt.

Analogously, we can derive that

\mathbb{P}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\hskip 0.50003pt\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{B}_{n}^{\text{MB}}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathbf{Y})-b\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\leq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right]\,\geq\,1-\frac{\beta}{2}

and since by
[
42
, Theorem 20]
the Hausdorff distance between two nonempty, compact, and convex sets can be reduced to their boundaries, we have
d_{H}(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{D}_{\hskip-0.2048ptn}^{\text{MB}},D)\,=\,\max\hskip 1.00006pt\{\lvert\mathbf{A}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}^{\hskip-1.22911pt\text{MB}}-a\rvert,\lvert\mathbf{B}_{n}^{\text{MB}}-b\rvert\}
.
Overall this implies that

\mathbb{P}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\hskip 0.50003ptd_{H}(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{D}_{\hskip-0.2048ptn}^{\text{MB}},D)\leq c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right]\,\geq\,\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)+\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)-1\,=\,1-\beta\hskip 1.00006pt,

such that by

c\,\leq\,6\hskip 0.50003pt(b-a)\hskip-1.49994pt\left(\sqrt{x}+(1+\sqrt{x})^{2}\right)\,\leq\,6\hskip 0.50003pt(b-a)(2+\sqrt{x})^{2}

the claim follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

We set
L=b-a
.
Then, for
t\in[0,L]
we have

\mathbb{P}[\mathbf{A}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}^{\hskip-1.22911pt\text{CH}}-a>t]\,=\,\mathbb{P}[\mathbf{Y}_{i}>t+a\text{ for all }i\in[n]]\,=\,\left(\frac{b-(a+t)}{L}\right)^{\hskip-2.04861ptn}\,=\,\left(1-\frac{t}{L}\right)^{\hskip-2.04861ptn}\hskip-1.99997pt,

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Using that
(1-x)^{n}\leq\exp(-nx)
for all
x\in[0,1]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
this then yields that

\mathbb{P}[\mathbf{A}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}^{\hskip-1.22911pt\text{CH}}-a>t]\,\leq\,\exp\hskip-1.00006pt\left(-\frac{n\hskip 0.50003ptt}{L}\right)\hskip-1.00006pt,

such that

\mathbb{P}[\mathbf{A}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}^{\hskip-1.22911pt\text{CH}}-a>c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{-r}]\,\leq\,\exp\hskip-1.99997pt\left(-\frac{c}{L}n^{1-r}\right)\,\eqqcolon\,\exp\left(-C\hskip 0.50003ptn^{s}\right)\hskip 0.50003pt,

where
C=c/L
and
s=1-r
.
Analogously we find that

\mathbb{P}[\hskip 0.50003ptb-\mathbf{B}_{n}^{\text{CH}}>c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{-r}]\,\leq\,\exp\left(-C\hskip 0.50003ptn^{s}\right)

and since by
[
42
, Theorem 20]
the Hausdorff distance between two nonempty, compact, and convex sets can be reduced to their boundaries we have
d_{H}(\hskip 0.35004pt\mathbf{D}_{\hskip-0.2048ptn}^{\text{CH}},D)\,=\,\max\hskip 1.00006pt\{\mathbf{A}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}^{\hskip-1.22911pt\text{CH}}-a,b-\mathbf{B}_{n}^{\text{CH}}\}
, such that

\mathbb{P}[d_{H}(\mathbf{D}_{\hskip-0.2048ptn}^{\text{CH}},D)>c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{-r}]\,\leq\,\mathbb{P}[\max\hskip 1.00006pt\{\mathbf{A}_{\hskip 0.12291ptn}^{\hskip-1.22911pt\text{CH}}-a,b-\mathbf{B}_{n}^{\text{CH}}\}>c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{-r}]\,\leq\,2\exp\left(-C\hskip 0.50003ptn^{s}\right)\hskip 1.00006pt.

Using the union bound we can then obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\hskip 0.50003ptd_{H}(\mathbf{D}_{\hskip-0.2048ptn}^{\text{CH}},D)>c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for some }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,\leq\,\sum_{n\in\hskip 0.40974pt\mathbb{N}}\,\mathbb{P}[\hskip 0.50003ptd_{H}(\mathbf{D}_{\hskip-0.2048ptn}^{\text{CH}},D)>c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,\leq\,2\hskip-1.00006pt\sum_{n\in\hskip 0.40974pt\mathbb{N}}\,\exp(-Cn^{s})\hskip 1.00006pt,

(100)

where the last sum in (
100
) converges if and only if
C>0
and
s>0
, which is equivalent to
c>0
and
r<1
.
We can now proceed similarly as in the proof of Lemma
B.4
to obtain that

\mathbb{P}[\hskip 0.50003ptd_{H}(\mathbf{D}_{\hskip-0.2048ptn}^{\text{CH}},D)>c\hskip 0.50003ptn^{\hskip-0.81949pt-r}\text{ for some }n\in\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,\leq\,\beta\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad c\,\geq\,(b-a)\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\frac{2\hskip 0.50003pt\Gamma(1/s)}{s\hskip 0.50003pt\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\hskip-2.04861pts}\hskip-1.00006pt,

(101)

such that the claim follows by taking the complement of the event in (
101
).
∎

Data -Driven Distributionally Robust Linear Quadratic Control Problem.

For the rest of this section let always
d\in\{n,p\}
.
Similar to the definition of the restricted Gelbrich ball in (
67
), for
X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
and
\rho\geq 0
we can define the restricted Gelbrich ball

\mathbb{G}(\hskip-0.50003ptX,\rho)\,=\,\left\{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}\,\colon d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)^{2}\leq\rho^{2}\text{ and }Y\succeq\lambda_{\text{min}}(X)I\right\}.

First, we show that the candidate for a domain approximation process
\mathbf{D}^{\mathbb{G}}
defined as in (
68
) is a well-defined stochastic process. To this end, we have to derive some bounds on the Gelbrich distance and the Frobenius norm with respect to each other.
Note that it is already well-known that

\lVert X\rVert_{2}\,\leq\,\lVert X\rVert_{F}\,\leq\,\trace(X)\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\lVert X\rVert_{F}\,\leq\,d\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert X\rVert_{2}

(102)

and consider the following result.

Lemma 21

Let
X,Y\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
.
Then, it holds

\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\sqrt{\lVert X\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right)\hskip-1.00006ptd_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)\quad\text{ and }\quad d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 1.00006pt.

Proof.
.

For the first bound, we use the representation

d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)\,=\,\min\left\{\left\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptX^{1/2}-Y^{1/2}\hskip 0.24994ptU\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rVert_{2}\colon U\in O(d)\right\}

(103)

that can be found in
[
9
, Theorem 1]
, where
O(d)
stands for the orthogonal group.
Let
U^{\star}\in O(d)
be a minimizer of (
103
) and write
P=X^{1/2}
and
Q=Y^{1/2}\hskip 0.24994ptU^{\star}
.
Then, we have
X=PP^{\top}
and
Y=Q\hskip 1.00006ptQ^{\top}
, such that with

X-Y\,=\,PP^{\top}\hskip-1.00006pt-Q\hskip 1.00006ptQ^{\top}\,=\,P(P-Q)^{\top}\hskip-1.00006pt-(P-Q)Q^{\top}

and (
102
) we obtain

\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\lVert X-Y\rVert_{2}\,=\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip-0.50003pt\left\lVert P(P-Q)^{\top}\hskip-1.00006pt-(P-Q)Q^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt(\lVert P\rVert_{2}+\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptQ\rVert_{2})\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert P-Q\rVert_{2}\hskip 1.00006pt.

Hence, noting that
\lVert P\rVert_{2}=\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptX\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{2}}
and
\lVert Q\rVert_{2}=\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptY\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{2}}
we obtain

\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\sqrt{\lVert X\rVert_{2}}+\sqrt{\lVert Y\rVert_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right)\hskip-1.99997pt\lVert P-Q\rVert_{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\sqrt{\lVert X\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right)\hskip-1.99997ptd_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)\hskip 1.00006pt,

where we used the optimality of
U^{\star}
in the second inequality.
For the second bound we again use (
102
) and the representation (
103
) to obtain

d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)\,\leq\,\left\lVert X^{1/2}-Y^{1/2}\right\rVert_{2}\,\leq\,\left\lVert X^{1/2}-Y^{1/2}\right\rVert_{F},

such that using the Powers -Størmer inequality
[
34
, Lemma 4.1]
immediately yields

d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)\,\leq\,\sqrt{\lVert X-Y\rVert_{*}}\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 1.00006pt,

where
\lVert\,\cdot\,\rVert_{*}
denotes the nuclear norm.
∎

Lemma 22

Let
\mathbf{Y}\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
be
\Sigma
-measurable and
\rho\geq 0
.
Then, the correspondence

\Omega\,\rightrightarrows\,\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd},\;\omega\,\mapsto\,\mathbb{G}(\mathbf{Y}(\omega),\rho)

has nonempty, compact, and convex values and is measurable.

Proof.
.

We consider the functions

g_{1}\colon\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}\times\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}\to\mathbb{R},\;(Y,X)\mapsto d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(Y,X)^{2}-\rho^{2}

and

g_{2}\colon\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}\times\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}\to\mathbb{R},\;(Y,X)\mapsto\lambda_{\min}(Y)-\lambda_{\min}(X)\hskip 1.00006pt.

Note that it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}(\mathbf{Y}\hskip-0.50003pt(\omega),\rho)

\displaystyle=\,\{X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}\,\colon d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\mathbf{Y}\hskip-0.50003pt(\omega),X)^{2}\leq\rho^{2}\text{ and }X\succeq\lambda_{\text{min}}(\mathbf{Y}\hskip-0.50003pt(\omega))I\}

\displaystyle=\,\{X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}\,\colon g_{1}(\mathbf{Y}\hskip-0.50003pt(\omega),X)\leq 0\text{ and }g_{2}(\mathbf{Y}\hskip-0.50003pt(\omega),X)\leq 0\}

\displaystyle=\,\mathbb{S}_{g}(\mathbf{Y}\hskip-0.50003pt(\omega),0)

for all
\omega\in\Omega
, such that it suffices to show that
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
satisfy the assumptions of Lemma
3.4
.
Since
X\mapsto\trace(X)
and
X\mapsto X^{1/2}
are continuous on
\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
the same holds for the Gelbrich distance
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}
in both arguments, such that
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}
is jointly continuous and hence a Carathéodory function.
Furthermore, by Lemma
B.4
we have

d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(Y,X)\,\geq\,\frac{\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}}{d\hskip 0.50003pt(\hskip-0.24994pt\sqrt{\lVert X\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 0.50003pt)}\,\geq\,\frac{\lVert X\rVert_{F}-\lVert Y\rVert_{F}}{d\hskip 0.50003pt(\hskip-0.24994pt\sqrt{\lVert X\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 0.50003pt)}\,\geq\,\frac{1}{d}(\sqrt{\lVert X\rVert_{F}}-\sqrt{\lVert Y\rVert_{F}})

for all
X,Y\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
, such that
g_{1}
is coercive in the second argument.
Lastly, we have

g_{1}(X,X)\,=\,d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,X)^{2}-\rho^{2}\,=\,-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,0

for all
X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
and
X\mapsto g_{1}(Y,X)
is convex on
\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
for all
Y\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
since
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}
is jointly convex by
[
31
, Proposition 2.3]
.
Thus,
g_{1}
satisfies both (
34
) and (
35
) and we turn our attention to
g_{2}
.
By Weyl’s inequality we have that

\lambda_{\min}(Y)-\lambda_{\min}(X)\,\leq\,\lambda_{\max}(Y-X)\,\leq\,\lVert Y-X\rVert_{2}\,\leq\,\lVert Y-X\rVert_{F}

for all
X,Y\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
, such that
X\mapsto\lambda_{\text{min}}(X)
is 1-Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
. Hence, it follows that
g_{2}
is jointly continuous and therefore a Carathéodory function.
Furthermore, we have

g_{2}(X,X)\,=\,\lambda_{\text{min}}(X)-\lambda_{\text{min}}(X)\,=\,0

for all
X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
and by the representation

\lambda_{\text{min}}(X)\,=\,\min\{a^{\hskip-1.22911pt\top}\hskip-1.99997ptXa\,\colon\lVert\hskip 0.50003pta\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{2}=1\}

we can see that
\lambda_{\text{min}}
is concave on
\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
, such that
X\mapsto g_{2}(Y,X)
is convex on
\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
for all
Y\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}.

Thus,
g_{2}
satisfies (
35
) as well, such that overall Lemma
3.4
is applicable and the claim follows.
∎

Lemma 23

The map
\mathbf{D}^{\mathbb{G}}
defined as in (
68
) is a well-defined stochastic process.

Proof.
.

Let
t\in\mathbb{N}
be fixed.
By assumption, the covariance matrix collection approximation
\hat{\mathbf{Z}}^{(t)}
is
\Sigma
-measurable, such that each component
\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}
itself is
\Sigma
-measurable for all
k\in[\hskip 0.24994pt2\hskip 0.50003ptT+1\hskip 0.24994pt]
.
Hence, by Lemma
B.4
we have that the correspondence

\varphi_{k}^{(t)}\colon\Omega\,\rightrightarrows\,\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd},\;\omega\,\mapsto\,\mathbb{G}\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-0.50003pt\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}\hskip-1.00006pt(\omega),\rho\right)

has nonempty, compact, and convex values and is measurable for each
k\in[\hskip 0.24994pt2\hskip 0.50003ptT+1\hskip 0.24994pt]
.
Therefore, by Lemma
A
, we have that the product correspondence

\varphi^{(t)}\colon\Omega\,\rightrightarrows\,\left(\mathbb{S}_{+}^{n}\times\prod_{k\hskip 0.40974pt=\hskip 0.40974pt0}^{T-1}\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptn}\times\prod_{k\hskip 0.40974pt=\hskip 0.40974pt0}^{T-1}\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptp}\right)\hskip-1.99997pt,\;\omega\,\mapsto\,\prod_{k\hskip 0.40974pt=1}^{2\hskip 0.2048ptT+1}\mathbb{G}\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-0.50003pt\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}\hskip-1.00006pt(\omega),\rho\right)

has nonempty, compact, and convex values and is weakly measurable.
Since
\varphi^{(t)}
implicitly has closed values, by Theorem
A
, we can now identify
\varphi^{(t)}
as map from
\Omega
to
\mathscr{D}
, such that
\mathbf{D}_{t}=\varphi^{(t)}
.
Overall, the claim follows since
t\in\mathbb{N}
was arbitrary.
∎

Since by Lemma
B.4
we now know that we indeed have the domain approximation process
\mathbf{D}^{\mathbb{G}}
it only remains to show that its corresponding domain approximation sequence
(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathbb{G}})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
converges with respect to the Hausdorff distance.

Lemma 24

Let
X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
. Then, for all
t\geq 0
we have
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,X+tI)\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.24994ptt}
.

Proof.
.

Since
X
and
X+tI
commute, there exist an invertible matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and diagonal matrices
D,D_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that
X=S^{-1}\hskip-0.50003ptDS
and
X+tI=S^{-1}\hskip-0.50003ptD_{t}S
.
Therefore, we obtain that
\trace(X)=\trace(D)
,
\trace(X+tI)=\trace(D_{t})
and

\displaystyle\trace\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.99997pt\left((X+tI)^{1/2}X(X+tI)^{1/2}\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pt1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\trace\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.99997pt\left(S^{-1}\hskip-0.50003ptD_{t}^{1/2}SS^{-1}\hskip-0.50003ptDSS^{-1}\hskip-0.50003ptD_{t}^{1/2}S\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pt1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\trace\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.99997pt\left(S^{-1}\hskip-0.50003ptDD_{t}S\right)^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\trace\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006ptD^{1/2}D_{t}^{1/2}\right)\hskip-1.00006pt,

such that overall

\displaystyle d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,X+tI)^{2}

\displaystyle=\,\trace(X)+\trace(X+tI)-2\trace\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.99997pt\left((X+tI)^{1/2}X(X+tI)^{1/2}\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pt1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\trace(D)+\trace(D_{t})-2\trace\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006ptD^{1/2}D_{t}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\trace\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.99997pt\left(D^{1/2}-D_{t}^{1/2}\right)^{\hskip-0.81949pt2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\sum_{i\hskip 0.40974pt=1}^{d}\left(\sqrt{\hskip-0.50003pt\lambda_{i}(X)}-\sqrt{\hskip-0.50003pt\lambda_{i}(X)+t}\right)^{\hskip-0.81949pt2}\hskip-1.99997pt,

where
\lambda_{1}\hskip-0.50003pt(X),\ldots,\lambda_{d}(X)
are the eigenvalues of
X
.
Since
X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
we have
\lambda_{i}(X)\geq 0
for all
i\in[n]
and since

\left(\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{a+t}\hskip 0.50003pt\right)^{\hskip-0.40974pt2}\,=\,\frac{t^{2}}{\left(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{a+t}\hskip 0.50003pt\right)^{\hskip-0.40974pt2}}\,\leq\,t

for all
a\geq 0
this directly yields that
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,X+tI)^{2}\leq d\hskip 0.24994ptt
.
∎

For the following result we implicitly need the concept of geodesic metric spaces.

Definition 10  –
(Geodesic Metric Space)

A metric space
(\hskip-0.74997ptX,d_{X}\hskip-0.50003pt)
is called
geodesic
if for all
x,y\in X
there exists a curve
\gamma\colon[0,1]\to X
with
\gamma(0)=x
and
\gamma(1)=y
satisfying

d_{X}(\gamma(s),\gamma(t))\,=\,\lvert\hskip 0.50003pts-t\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert\hskip 1.00006ptd_{X}(x,y)\hskip 1.00006pt.

The curve
\gamma
then is called
minimizing geodesic
.

Note that the metric spaces
(\hskip 0.24994pt\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd},d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}\hskip-0.50003pt)
and
(\hskip 0.24994pt\mathbb{S}_{++}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd},d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}\hskip-0.50003pt)
are geodesic
[
9
,
40
]
.

Lemma 25

Let
X,Y\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
.
Then, for all
\rho\geq 0
we have

d_{H}(\mathbb{G}(X,\rho),\mathbb{G}(Y,\rho))\,\leq\,d\hskip 0.50003pt(d+2)\hskip-1.49994pt\left(2\rho+\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptX\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptY\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}}\right)\hskip-0.50003pt\sqrt{\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 1.00006pt.

Proof.
.

Let
\tilde{X}\in\mathbb{G}(X,\rho)
and set
t=\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\lambda_{\text{min}}(Y)-\lambda_{\text{min}}(\hskip-0.50003ptX)\hskip 0.50003pt\rvert
.
Then, by Lemma
B.4
and the triangle inequality for
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}
, we have

d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X}+tI,Y)\,\leq\,d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X}+tI,\tilde{X})+d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X},X)+d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.24994ptt\hskip 0.50003pt}+\rho+d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)

(104)

and furthermore

\tilde{X}+tI\,\succeq\,(\lambda_{\text{min}}(X)+t)I\,\succ\,\lambda_{\text{min}}(Y)I\hskip 1.00006pt.

If
\lambda_{\text{min}}(Y)=0
, then due to
[
40
]
we can find a minimizing geodesic
\gamma\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
with
\gamma(0)=\tilde{X}+tI
and
\gamma(1)=Y
.
Denoting
\varepsilon=d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)+\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.24994ptt\hskip 0.50003pt}
and
\tilde{Y}=\gamma(\varepsilon/(\rho+\varepsilon))
we then find

d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{Y},Y)\,=\,\left\lvert\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho+\varepsilon}-1\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rvert\hskip 1.00006ptd_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X}+tI,Y)\,\leq\,\frac{\rho}{\rho+\varepsilon}(\rho+\varepsilon)\,=\,\rho\hskip 1.00006pt,

(105)

where we used (
104
) in the inequality.
Hence,
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{Y},Y)\leq\rho
and
\tilde{Y}\succeq 0=\lambda_{\text{min}}(Y)I
such that
\tilde{Y}\in\mathbb{G}(Y,\rho)
.
If
\lambda_{\text{min}}(Y)>0
, then due to
[
9
, Section 4]
we may choose the minimizing geodesic
\gamma\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{S}_{++}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}
with

\gamma(s)\,=\,s^{2}(\tilde{X}+tI)+(1-s)^{2}\hskip 0.24994ptY+s\hskip 0.24994pt(1-s)\hskip-1.00006pt\left(\hskip-1.99997pt\left((\tilde{X}+tI)Y\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pt1/2}\hskip-1.00006pt+\left(Y(\tilde{X}+tI)\right)^{\hskip-1.63885pt1/2}\right)

for which it is easy to check that
\gamma(s)\succeq\lambda_{\text{min}}(Y)I
for all
s\in[0,1]
, such that in this case
\tilde{Y}\in\mathbb{G}(Y,\rho)
as well.
Analogously to (
105
) we find that
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X}+tI,\tilde{Y})\leq\varepsilon
, such that with

d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X},\tilde{Y})\,\leq\,d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X},\tilde{X}+tI)+d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X}+tI,\tilde{Y})\,\leq\,d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)+2\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.24994ptt\hskip 0.50003pt}

and Lemma
B.4
we have

\lVert\tilde{X}-\tilde{Y}\rVert_{F}\,\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt\sqrt{\lVert\tilde{X}\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert\tilde{Y}\rVert_{F}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right)\hskip-1.99997pt\left(d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,Y)+2\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.24994ptt\hskip 0.50003pt}\right)\hskip-1.00006pt.

(106)

By the triangle inequality for
d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}
we further have

\sqrt{\lVert\tilde{X}\rVert_{F}}\,\leq\,\sqrt{\trace(\tilde{X})}\,=\,d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X},0)\,\leq\,d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(\tilde{X},X)+d_{\hskip 0.2048ptG}(X,0)\,\leq\,\rho+\sqrt{\trace(X)}\,\leq\,\rho+\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert X\rVert_{F}}

and analogously

\sqrt{\lVert\tilde{Y}\rVert_{F}}\,\leq\,\rho+\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 1.00006pt.

Hence, considering (
106
), together with Lemma
B.4
and the 1-Lipschitz continuity of
\lambda_{\text{min}}
on
\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\hskip 0.2048ptd}

we obtain that

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{X}-\tilde{Y}\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\,\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-1.99997pt\left(2\rho+\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert X\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right)\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\sqrt{\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}}+2\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\,d\hskip 0.50003pt(d+2)\hskip-1.49994pt\left(2\rho+\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptX\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptY\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}}\right)\hskip-0.50003pt\sqrt{\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 1.00006pt,

where we used that
1+2\sqrt{\hskip-1.00006ptd\hskip 0.50003pt}\leq d+2
for all
d\in\mathbb{N}
in the second inequality.
Since the right hand side does not depend on
\tilde{Y}
anymore this yields that

\displaystyle\sup\hskip-1.00006pt\left\{h(\tilde{X},\mathbb{G}(Y,\rho))\,\colon\tilde{X}\in\mathbb{G}(X,\rho)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\,\sup\hskip-1.00006pt\left\{\lVert\tilde{X}-\tilde{Y}\rVert_{F}\,\colon\tilde{X}\in\mathbb{G}(X,\rho)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\,d\hskip 0.50003pt(d+2)\hskip-1.49994pt\left(2\rho+\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptX\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptY\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}}\right)\hskip-0.50003pt\sqrt{\lVert X-Y\rVert_{F}}\hskip 1.00006pt.

The claim now follows by symmetry in the arguments.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.
.

Let
\omega\in\Omega
be a fixed sample point corresponding to the event in (
69
).
Then, there exists some
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
k\in[2\hskip 0.50003ptT+1]
we have

\lVert\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}-Z_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}\,\leq\,\eta+1\hskip 1.00006pt,

implying that

\lVert\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}\rVert_{F}\,\leq\,\eta+1+\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptZ_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}

for all
t\geq N
.
Hence, using Lemma
B.4
we find that

d_{H}(\mathbb{G}(\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}\hskip-1.00006pt(\omega),\rho),\mathbb{G}(Z_{k},\rho))\,\leq\,M_{k}\hskip 0.24994pt\lVert\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}\hskip-1.00006pt(\omega)-Z_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}^{1/2}

for all
t\geq N
, where

M_{k}\,\coloneqq\,d_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt(d_{k}+2)\hskip-1.49994pt\left(2\rho+\sqrt{\eta+1+\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptZ_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}}+\sqrt{\lVert\hskip 0.50003ptZ_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}}\right)

for all
k\in[2\hskip 0.50003ptT+1]
.
Since the Hausdorff distance of a product of sets is bounded above by the sum of the Hausdorff distance of each component, we furthermore obtain that

\displaystyle d_{H}(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}\hskip-0.50003pt\hskip-0.50003pt(\omega),D)\,\leq\,\sum_{k\hskip 0.40974pt=1}^{2\hskip 0.2048ptT+1}d_{H}(\mathbb{G}(\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}\hskip-1.00006pt(\omega),\rho),\mathbb{G}(Z_{k},\rho))\,\leq\,\sum_{k\hskip 0.40974pt=1}^{2\hskip 0.2048ptT+1}M_{k}\hskip 0.24994pt\lVert\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}\hskip-1.00006pt(\omega)-Z_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}^{1/2}\hskip 1.00006pt,

such that

\limsup_{t\hskip 0.40974pt\to\hskip 0.40974pt\infty}\hskip 2.5ptd_{H}(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}\hskip-0.50003pt(\omega),D)\,\leq\,\limsup_{t\hskip 0.40974pt\to\hskip 0.40974pt\infty}\hskip 2.5pt\sum_{k\hskip 0.40974pt=1}^{2\hskip 0.2048ptT+1}M_{k}\hskip 0.24994pt\lVert\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{k}^{(t)}\hskip-1.00006pt(\omega)-Z_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\rVert_{F}^{1/2}\,\leq\,\underbrace{\left(\hskip 0.74997pt\sum_{k\hskip 0.40974pt=1}^{2\hskip 0.2048ptT+1}M_{k}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)}_{M\coloneqq}\hskip-1.49994pt\sqrt{\eta\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip 1.00006pt.

The claim now follows since the probability of the event in (
69
) by assumption is at least
1-\beta
.
∎
